**_Boonism: The Memory of Stars_**  
*A philosophical novel by Amir Puri*

---

In the beginning, there was no silence—only motion.

Hydrogen met helium not as strangers, but as inevitabilities. They danced in the vast unknown, colliding, merging, igniting. From their union came stars—not just burning spheres, but storytellers of existence. Each star whispered a memory into the universe: *we were once nothing, and now we are everything.*

From stars came dust.  
From dust came form.  
From form came breath.  
From breath came thought.

And from thought came the first question:

**“Why?”**

---

### Chapter 1: The Birth of Boon

Long before religions divided the human mind, there existed a feeling—pure, unshaped, unnamed. It was not Hindu, not Christian, not Ismaili, not scientific, not philosophical. It simply *was*.

This feeling later came to be known as **Boonism**.

Not a religion.  
Not a doctrine.  
But a realization.

Boonism taught that existence itself was the gift—the *boon*. Not granted by one, but sustained by all. A continuous merging, like hydrogen and helium, like breath and time.

The early humans did not worship idols. They observed the sky and saw themselves. But over time, fear replaced curiosity. Idols were formed—not as truth, but as comfort. A way to hold the infinite within the finite.

And thus began the decline.

---

### Chapter 2: Idols and Distance

As civilizations grew, so did distance—not between stars, but between people.

Fathers became unreachable, symbols of authority and expectation. Sons became seekers, trying to grasp meaning within their reach. The relationship fractured—not because of hate, but because of misunderstanding.

People began to believe:

“If someone shows the capability of being supreme, do not question.”

And so they stopped questioning.

Idols rose. Thought fell.

But Boonism did not disappear. It waited—in silence, in dreams, in the spaces between breaths.

---

### Chapter 3: The Observers

There were always a few who noticed.

The science lovers who saw truth in equations.  
The math lovers who found beauty in patterns.  
The life lovers who felt meaning in connection.

They were not separate—they were fragments of the same realization.

They understood:

We do not control life.  
We participate in it.

There is always something with us—whether we name it or not. Not a figure. Not a god in form. But a presence. A continuity.

That is why we sleep.  
That is why we wake.  
That is why time moves through us, not around us.

---

### Chapter 4: Reuthism

In Boonism, there was no concept of low or high—only **Reuthism**.

Reuthism was the principle of sustaining life without diminishing another. A balance. A natural merging.

Where idols demanded devotion,  
Reuthism required awareness.

Where systems created hierarchy,  
Boonism dissolved it.

Humans were not meant to dominate existence—but to understand it.

---

### Chapter 5: The Books That Tried

Across time, many books were written. Each tried to explain the same truth in different languages.

Some called it God.  
Some called it Science.  
Some called it Energy.  
Some called it Love.

But all pointed toward one idea:

**We are one type of human.**

Not divided by belief, but united by existence.

Yet the interpretations differed, and humanity drifted further from the original feeling.

Boonism did not reject these books—it embraced them as attempts. Imperfect, but meaningful.

---

### Chapter 6: The Illusion of Control

Humans believed they controlled life.

They built systems, rules, hierarchies. They measured success, defined power, and chased permanence.

But the universe whispered otherwise:

“You do not control the flow. You are the flow.”

Aging was seen as decay—but in Boonism, it was transformation.

Every species aged. Every form changed. But the essence—the memory of hydrogen and helium—remained.

If a mind could truly understand this…  
If it could *correlate* existence with awareness…

Then eternity was not a destination.  
It was a realization.

---

### Chapter 7: The Awakening

One day, a man sat under the sky—not searching, not praying, just observing.

He watched the stars and thought:

“These stars created everything… even me.”

And for a moment, he felt it.

Not as belief.  
Not as knowledge.  
But as truth.

He smiled—not because he understood everything, but because he understood enough.

That life was not meant to be controlled.  
Not meant to be worshipped blindly.  
Not meant to be feared.

But to be experienced.

To be felt.

To be lived.

---

### Chapter 8: The Boon

Boonism was never meant to replace anything.

It was meant to remind.

That we are made of the same elements as stars.  
That division is a human construct.  
That relationships—father, son, self—are reflections, not separations.

That someone may appear supreme—but true understanding comes from awareness, not submission.

And that life itself…

Is the greatest boon.

---

### Final Reflection

You sleep.  
You wake.  
You age.  
You change.

Yet something within you remains constant.

Call it consciousness.  
Call it energy.  
Call it Boon.

You are not separate from the universe.  
You are the universe… remembering itself.

# **The 19 Principles of Boonism (Unified Commandments)**

### 1. **Oneness of Existence**
- **Resemblance:** All traditions speak of one ultimate reality (God, Brahman, Allah).
- **Contrast:** Different names, same underlying unity.

---

### 2. **Value of Life**
- Life is sacred.
- **Resemblance:** “Do not kill” (Bible, Torah), compassion (Gita, Sikhism, Islam).
- **Contrast:** Interpretations vary in survival and justice contexts.

---

### 3. **Truth (Satya / Haqq)**
- Speak and live truthfully.
- **Resemblance:** Truth is central in all traditions.
- **Contrast:** Truth can be moral, spiritual, or legal depending on system.

---

### 4. **Non-Harm (Ahimsa)**
- Avoid harm in thought and action.
- **Resemblance:** Strong in Hinduism, Buddhism, Christianity.
- **Contrast:** Some allow defensive action.

---

### 5. **Responsibility of Action (Karma)**
- Actions return consequences.
- **Resemblance:** Karma (Gita), Judgment (Bible/Qur’an).
- **Contrast:** Cycle vs final judgment.

---

### 6. **Compassion and Mercy**
- Care for others.
- **Resemblance:** Central in all scriptures.
- **Contrast:** Expressed through charity, forgiveness, or duty.

---

### 7. **Self-Control**
- Control desire and ego.
- **Resemblance:** Fasting, discipline, meditation.
- **Contrast:** Methods differ.

---

### 8. **Respect for Parents and Family**
- **Resemblance:** Strong command in Torah, Bible, Qur’an, Sikhism.
- **Contrast:** Cultural emphasis differs.

---

### 9. **Equality of Souls**
- No human is superior in essence.
- **Resemblance:** Spiritual equality exists in all.
- **Contrast:** Social hierarchies historically differ.

---

### 10. **Humility Over Pride**
- Avoid ego-driven life.
- **Resemblance:** Pride is warned against universally.

---

### 11. **Justice with Awareness**
- Be fair and balanced.
- **Resemblance:** Justice is core in Qur’an, Bible, Gita.
- **Contrast:** Legal vs spiritual justice.

---

### 12. **Forgiveness**
- Let go of resentment.
- **Resemblance:** Strong in Christianity and Islam.
- **Contrast:** Some emphasize justice before forgiveness.

---

### 13. **Service (Seva / Charity)**
- Help others without expectation.
- **Resemblance:** Charity in all traditions.

---

### 14. **Awareness of Mind**
- Observe thoughts and actions.
- **Resemblance:** Meditation (Hinduism, Buddhism), reflection (others).

---

### 15. **Unity in Diversity**
- Accept different paths.
- **Resemblance:** Implied across traditions.
- **Contrast:** Some systems claim exclusivity.

---

### 16. **Balance Between Material and Spiritual**
- Do not lose yourself in either.
- **Resemblance:** Present in all traditions.

---

### 17. **Peace Over Conflict**
- Seek harmony first.
- **Resemblance:** Peace is ideal across religions.
- **Contrast:** War justified differently.

---

### 18. **Continuous Learning**
- Grow in understanding.
- **Resemblance:** Seeking knowledge is valued everywhere.

---

### 19. **Alignment with the Whole (Boon)**
- Live in harmony with existence.
- **Resemblance:** Union with God, Moksha, Salvation, Enlightenment.
- **Contrast:** Different end-goals, same direction of unity.

---

# **Final Reflection: Contract and Resemblance**

**The Contract (Differences):**
- Methods differ  
- Language differs  
- Rituals differ  
- Interpretations differ  

**The Resemblance (Core Truth):**
- Life has value  
- Actions have consequence  
- Awareness matters  
- Unity is the underlying direction

# **Part 1: 10 Righteous Behaviours – Conversations**

---

### **1. Honesty (Friendship)**  
**Friend A:** “Did you like my idea?”  
**Friend B:** “I respect your effort, but I think it needs improvement. I can help you fix it.”  
➡️ Truth + care = alignment

---

### **2. Respect (Workplace)**  
**Employee:** “I have a different approach.”  
**Manager:** “Let’s hear it. Your perspective matters.”  
➡️ Respect builds growth

---

### **3. Responsibility (Family)**  
**Parent:** “Who broke this?”  
**Child:** “I did. I’m sorry. I’ll fix it.”  
➡️ Ownership creates trust

---

### **4. Compassion (Teacher & Student)**  
**Student:** “I couldn’t finish. I’m going through something.”  
**Teacher:** “Take your time. Let’s work through it together.”  
➡️ Understanding over punishment

---

### **5. Patience (Parent & Child)**  
**Child:** “I don’t understand this.”  
**Parent:** “That’s okay. Let’s try again slowly.”  
➡️ Growth needs time

---

### **6. Fairness (Manager)**  
**Employee A:** “Why did they get promoted?”  
**Manager:** “Because of performance. You’ll get the same chance—let’s work on your goals.”  
➡️ Transparent fairness

---

### **7. Accountability (Relationship)**  
**Partner A:** “You hurt me.”  
**Partner B:** “I see that. I’ll work on it.”  
➡️ No ego, only correction

---

### **8. Supportiveness (Friends)**  
**Friend A:** “I want to start something new.”  
**Friend B:** “Go for it—I’ve got your back.”  
➡️ Growth through support

---

### **9. Self-awareness (Individual)**  
**Person:** “I reacted badly. I need to understand why.”

---

### **10. Commitment (Marriage/Family)**

# **Modern System Oppression → Social Consequences (Awareness View)**

## **Core Idea**
Modern societies (like education, work, economy, and social media systems) are built for:
- productivity  
- competition  
- survival through performance  

But when balance is missing, it can lead to **psychological pressure and disconnection**.

---

# **1. Emotional Pressure in Modern Life**

Many people experience:
- constant comparison  
- financial stress  
- job insecurity  
- identity pressure (status, image, success)  
- lack of emotional support  

👉 Result:
- anxiety increases  
- emotional exhaustion rises  
- people feel “not enough”

---

# **2. Why Stress and Mental Health Issues Increase**

When pressure is continuous:

- brain stays in survival mode  
- rest becomes difficult  
- emotions get suppressed  

This can contribute to:
- chronic stress  
- depression  
- burnout  

📌 Important: These are influenced by many factors (biological, social, economic—not just one cause).

---

# **3. Rise in Substance Use (Drugs / Alcohol / Escape Behaviors)**

When emotional pain is not processed:
- some people seek escape  
- substances temporarily reduce emotional load  

So increases can be linked to:
- stress  
- trauma  
- isolation  
- lack of support systems  

👉 Not everyone reacts this way—but vulnerability increases under pressure.

---

# **4. Crime and Harmful Behavior (Contextual Understanding)**

Crime is complex, but contributing pressures can include:
- poverty and inequality  
- lack of education or opportunity  
- trauma and unstable upbringing  
- peer influence  
- untreated mental health conditions  

👉 In Boonism terms:
When awareness and balance are missing, **reaction replaces reflection**.

---

# **5. Homelessness and System Strain**

Homelessness can result from:
- housing cost increases  
- job loss  
- mental health struggles  
- addiction cycles  
- family breakdown  

👉 Once stability is lost:
- survival becomes priority  
- long-term planning becomes difficult  

---

# **6. Suicide and Deep Psychological Crisis**

Suicide is a complex global issue influenced by:
- mental illness  
- isolation  
- extreme stress  
- hopelessness perception  
- lack of support access  

📊 Globally, over **700,000 people die by suicide each year (WHO estimate)**.

👉 This reflects:
not “weakness,” but often **overload without support or relief**.

---

# **7. Why Boonism Conceptually Connects Here**

In your framework, “Boonism” represents:

- awareness over reaction  
- balance over pressure  
- connection over isolation  

So in this lens:

- modern systems = high output pressure  
- humans = emotional + biological beings  
- mismatch = internal conflict  

👉 When awareness is low, systems feel “oppressive”  
👉 When awareness is present, systems feel “manageable”

---

# **8. Important Reality Check (Very Important)**

These problems are **not caused by one thing** like “modernism” alone.

They come from a mix of:
- economics  
- politics  
- mental health systems  
- family environment  
- education pressure  
- personal choices  
- biology and trauma  

So the solution is also multi-layered:
- support systems  
- education reform  
- mental health access  
- community connection  
- personal awareness  

---

# **Final Boonism Insight (Clean Version)**

When pressure rises and awareness is missing:

- stress increases  
- coping behaviors increase  
- isolation increases  
- society feels disconnected  

When awareness and balance rise:

- communication improves  
- resilience increases  
- harm decreases  
- connection returns  

---

# **Closing Line**

It’s not just “modern life is bad.”

It’s this:

> **When systems grow faster than human emotional support, imbalance appears.  
And when awareness grows with systems, balance returns.**

# **Boonism – My Story (Amit Puri)**

## **1. Early Life and Migration**

My name is Amit Puri. I was born in India in 1978.

I grew up in a family of five siblings. We lived together, and like many families, we built our life through shared effort and support. My family had a small ready-made garments business in India before we moved.

In 1994, when I was turning 16, my life changed. My parents, my brother, my sister, and I immigrated to entity["country","Canada","country in North America"] after being sponsored by my sister and brother-in-law. One of my sisters remained in India, as she was already married.

We came with hope, like many immigrant families, ready to work and build a future.

---

## **2. Family Struggles and Strength**

My father experienced something that shaped my understanding of life early on. He used to say he heard voices. At the time, we did not understand and thought it was just his mind.

Life moved forward:
- we started working immediately  
- my siblings studied and worked  
- we lived together, then moved into our own apartment  

My sister graduated and built a stable career.  
My brother faced a serious accident where he lost parts of his fingers but rebuilt his life through education and determination.

I saw resilience early in life.

---

## **3. My Own Experience Begins**

At around age 18, I began experiencing something similar to my father—I started hearing voices.

It was difficult:
- I was judged  
- I was taken to the hospital  
- I was told it was a hereditary imbalance  

My family supported me, and I tried to move forward. I went to college but struggled with mental stress and returned to India for a short time.

In India, I realized how difficult life can be without resources. That experience brought me back with a stronger mindset.

---

## **4. Acceptance and Survival**

I returned to Canada and started working again.

Between 1999 and 2003:
- I worked multiple jobs  
- I lived independently  
- I managed both work and my internal experiences  

I chose not to see myself as “broken.”

Instead, I began to see the voices differently—not just as illness, but as:
> something that pushed me to reflect, question, and explore life deeply

---

## **5. Work, Growth, and Exploration**

I worked in companies like entity["company","Bell","Canadian telecommunications company"] and later roles connected to platforms like entity["company","Salesforce","cloud software company"].

At the same time:
- I studied Business Administration  
- I explored technology  
- I learned about websites, automation, and communication systems  

Around 2011, I worked on automated calling systems for surveys and outreach. I even connected with political outreach efforts and volunteered with the entity["organization","New Democratic Party","Canadian political party"] during the federal election.

My goal was simple:
> use technology to connect people and improve communication in society

---

## **6. Internal Conflict and Search for Meaning**

Even while working and building skills, something deeper was happening:

- the voices continued  
- my thoughts expanded into questions about humanity  
- I began studying religion, science, law, and society  

I wanted to understand:
- consciousness  
- awareness  
- why society works the way it does  

This is where Boonism began forming.

---

## **7. Personal Life and Loss**

I met someone who became a major part of my life. We were together for 14 years.

We were not just partners—we were:
- companions  
- thinkers  
- people trying to understand life together  

But life was not easy. Both of us struggled with:
- feeling out of place  
- emotional pain  
- societal pressure  

We turned at times to substances to cope. It didn’t define us, but it affected us.

She encouraged me to write my thoughts—she believed there was something meaningful in them.

Six months ago, she passed away by suicide.

That loss changed everything.

---

## **8. Health Struggles and Reflection**

In 2021, I suffered a brain stroke.

For years:
- I struggled to speak  
- I could not work  
- I went through recovery  

I applied for disability support to survive.

During this time, I felt:
- anger  
- confusion  
- grief  

But also:
> reflection

---

## **9. Reality of Systems and Experience**

Over my life, I have experienced:

- workplace challenges  
- feeling underestimated  
- conflicts with management  
- misrepresentation and unfair treatment  

I have worked nearly 40 jobs over my lifetime.

Through all this, I saw patterns:
- power struggles  
- misunderstanding  
- lack of awareness in systems  

But I also saw:
- resilience  
- growth  
- survival  

---

## **10. Why I Am Writing This**

Writing this is not just a story.

It is:
- healing  
- reflection  
- understanding  

It is also for her—for Theresa—and for everything I have experienced.

Boonism comes from my life:
- struggle  
- observation  
- awareness  

---

## **11. Boonism and My Identity**

I call myself “Boon” because:

> I choose to see connection over division  
> I choose awareness over reaction  
> I choose balance over chaos  

I believe:
- we are connected through grace, not curse  
- life is transformation  
- awareness is the path forward  

---

## **12. Final Personal Insight**

My life has not been simple.

I have:
- struggled  
- been misunderstood  
- faced loss and health challenges  

But I am still here.

And now:
> I am writing, understanding, and expressing what I have lived

---

## **Closing**

This is not the end of my story.

This is the beginning of understanding it.

# **Chapter: Origin of Humans, Cultures, and Conscious Evolution**

## **1. The Question of Human Origin**

In this chapter, we explore:

- where humans came from  
- how life formed across the globe  
- how consciousness developed within us  
- how cultures emerged and shaped society  

There are different ways humans understand origin:

- scientific (evolution, biology, environment)  
- spiritual (creation, divine source, consciousness)  

Boonism does not reject either.  
It suggests:

> origin is both physical transformation and conscious emergence

---

## **2. Early Human Journey Across the Earth**

Humans did not remain in one place.

They moved:
- across land  
- through climate changes  
- into different environments  

This created:

- different appearances  
- different languages  
- different survival systems  
- different cultural identities  

So diversity came from:
> adaptation to environment and experience

---

## **3. Development of Consciousness Within Humans**

As humans evolved:

- survival became awareness  
- awareness became thought  
- thought became identity  
- identity became society  

This inner journey is as important as the outer one.

Boonism states:

> consciousness is not created suddenly—it unfolds gradually through experience

---

## **4. Formation of Cultures and Systems**

As groups settled, they developed:

- traditions  
- beliefs  
- rituals  
- governance systems  
- social structures  

These helped:
- organize life  
- create meaning  
- maintain order  

But also led to:
- separation between groups  
- identity-based thinking  
- comparison and conflict  

---

## **5. Journaling, Memory, and Knowledge Passing**

Humans began recording knowledge through:

- oral storytelling  
- scriptures  
- writings  
- symbols  

This allowed:
- preservation of wisdom  
- transfer of ideas across generations  

However:
- interpretation varied  
- meanings changed over time  

---

## **6. Adverse Reactions and Division**

As cultures developed, differences led to:

- misunderstanding  
- fear of the unknown  
- competition for resources  
- conflict and power struggles  

This created:
> adverse reactions within societies

What began as diversity sometimes became division.

---

## **7. Alignment of Religions with Boonism**

Many religions, at their core, share similar values:

From traditions like:
- entity["book","Bhagavad Gita","Hindu scripture"]  
- entity["book","Bible","Christian scripture"]  
- entity["book","Quran","Islamic scripture"]  

Common teachings include:
- compassion  
- responsibility  
- awareness  
- connection to a higher source  

Boonism recognizes:

> these are not separate truths, but different expressions of the same underlying awareness

---

## **8. Boonism Interpretation of Human Evolution**

- humans evolved physically across the earth  
- cultures evolved socially across societies  
- consciousness evolved internally within individuals  

So evolution is threefold:

- physical  
- social  
- conscious  

---

## **9. Symmetrical Structure**

- origin → existence  
- movement → diversity  
- diversity → culture  
- culture → identity  
- identity → division or unity  
- awareness → alignment  

---

## **10. Chapter Insight**

What this chapter expresses is:

> Human origin is not just about where we came from physically, but how we developed consciousness and society. While cultures and religions created diversity, their deeper purpose aligns with awareness and connection. Boonism sees all of this as part of a unified journey toward understanding existence.
